1266. WILLIAM RATFORD was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of April, 4 shillings, the monies of William Briscoe.
ANN BRISCOE. I am the wife of William Briscoe - we keep the Rose and Crown, in Clare-court . The prisoner came in on the 17th of April, just as I had received a handful of silver from one of my servants; he came into my parlour, begged pardon, and asked if I would give him four sixpences for two shillings; I looked, and said I had only three - I asked my son for one; he then put his hand to my hand, apologized for touching me, and said, "You have a bad shilling, a lion and a lamb shilling," and he took up four shillings between his fingers; my son saw him do it, and took him into another room, where it was taken from him.
Cross-examined by MR. LEE. Q. I believe this was in the middle of the day? A. Yes, about eleven o'clock; I was not aware that I had a lion and lamb shilling in my hand - the prisoner did not give me the two shillings; I had counted the money in my hand; I had seventeen shillings and three sixpences.
WILLIAM SHELLEY. I am the prosecutrix's son. What my mother has stated is correct; I watched the prisoner when I saw him touch the money - I saw him secretly working four shillings between his fingers - I then got up, and watched his hand; when he was about bidding my mother good morning, I seized his hand, bent it back, and took him in the back parlour; the officer came, and took him - there were three shillings found in his hand, and one had dropped on the floor - the four sixpences were in his other hand.
Cross-examined. Q.Did you not charge him first of all with taking six shillings between his fingers? A. No, I am certain he had not this silver in his hand before he took it from my mother.
SAMUEL GARDNER (Police-constable T 98). I was sent for - I took the prisoner, and found three shillings in one of his hands; one had dropped on the floor - I found a pair of black gloves on him.
GUILTY. Aged 25. - Transported for Seven Years.

1274. JOSEPH THACTON and JOSEPH LIGHT were indicted for stealing, on the 2nd of May, 1 bag, value 2d.; 1 shilling, and 2 halfpence, the property of Thomas Enwright, from his person.
THOMAS ENWRIGHT. I am a sweep, and am thirteen years of age. On the 2nd of May I was sitting in Paddington-fields with another boy; we had a shilling andtwo halfpence in a bag; the prisoners came up to us -Thacton took the bag and money from me; he threw the bag into the water, and said there was the bag and money - Light then gave me the bag and the 1d. back again; I asked him for the shillings, but he would not give me that; they then got over the wall, into the Bayswater-road - my master had given me the shilling; I told an officer, and he took them - I am sure they are the persons: I had seen one of them walking about, but never spoke to him.
ROBERT GRIFFITH. I was with Enwright in the fields- I saw he had a bag in his hand, and he showed me a shilling and two halfpence in it; the prisoners came up together; Thacton took the bag from him, and gave it to Light - they then threw something into the water, and said there was the bag and money; they took the shilling out, and wanted to give the bag and the two halfpence back -Enwright said he would give them the penny if they would give him the shilling; they then got up, and we went to go after them - they said they would beat us - we saw an officer, and told him.
JAMES MARONA. I was selling some fruit on the Bays-water-road - I gave Light change for 1s., and he bought 1d. worth of nuts of me, on the 2nd of May, between six and seven o'clock in the evening.
WILLIAM THOMAS TURNER (Police-constable A 43). Enwright told me of this; I had seen the two prisoners pass me just before - I pursued, and took them at Knights-bridge; I found 9 1/2d. on them, part on each of them - the other 1 1/2d. Thacton said he had bought milk with.
Thacton's Defence. I saw the two boys gambling, and I took up the bag, which I carelessly threw to Light, but we took nothing out of it.
THACTON - GUILTY. Aged 17.
LIGHT - GUILTY. Aged 18.
Confined Six Weeks.

1275. SOLOMON TAYLOR was indicted for stealing, on the 19th of April, 1 coat, value 12s., the goods of Thomas Perkins.
THOMAS PERKINS. I am a gun-maker. I was at a skittle-ground in Bethnal-green, on the 19th of April, from dinner time till half-past four o'clock, next door to my own house; I had a light blue coat on, with a velvet collar - I took it off, and hung it on the pales between the two yards - I saw the prisoner there, with a black coat on; I missed my coat in about ten minutes or a quarter of an hour, and he was gone - I have never seen it since; I had seen the prisoner before.
JANE HITCHINS. My husband keeps the house. I saw the prisoner pass my bar with two coats on, a blue one undermost, and an old black one over it - they were both close bodies coats: I saw him come in with only one coat.
THOMAS MORFORD (Police-constable K 83.) I took the prisoner with an old dark blue coat on, with coloured buttons - it had a silk collar, not a velvet collar.
Prisoner's Defence. I put on two coats, as it was a wet day, and I had not been long out of a fever.
GUILTY. Aged 20. - Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18320517-150]
1276. JOHN TREADAWAY and MICHAEL LARKIN were indicted for stealing, on the 16th of April, 5 shillings, 1 sixpence, and 3 1/2d. in monies numbered, the monies of Charles Bolton, from his person.
CHARLES BOLTON. I go about gathering bones and old iron. I was out with a cart of my uncle's on the 16th of April, at Old Brentford; I went to the King's Arms, and when I came out I saw the prisoners, who asked me to give them a ride as far as the Star and Garter, and they said they would stand a pot of ale; I said, "What is the use of your getting up, my cart is not fit to carry you?" they said they were very tired, and I said, "Well, you may get up;" just before we got to the Star and Garter, Larkin said, "I have lost a sovereign;" I said, "Look for it in the cart, and then come and search me;" I went into the Star and Garter - they would not look in the cart, but they came into the house to me - I pulled out 5s. 9 1/2d., which was all I had; Treadaway took it up, put it into his pocket, and said, "I shall keep this;" I said, "You shall not:" I turned to lay hold of him, and Larkin knocked me down - they were going out; I went to stop Treadaway again, and they both fell upon me; I ran round the cart, and they followed and struck me again - they then ran away; I gave charge of them.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q.How long have you collected bones and iron? A. These fourteen years; there were ten or twelve persons in the house - the donkey I then had was my uncle's; I was tried at Reading once about a donkey, and was twelve months in gaol, but I was innocent.
WILLIAM PEASGOOD (Police-constable T 28). On the 16th of April I was on duty - the prosecutor said he had been robbed; I took Larkin against the distiller's, and he said, "This fellow (the prosecutor) has robbed me of a sovereign;" I said, "Come to the station, and I will search him;" he said, "Search him under the lamp;" I said No, and took him to the station - Larkin then said he had been with Cobby - I said I knew him - I went and found Treadaway at a public-house, the Running Horse, with two girls; he said, "What do you want?" I said,"Come out, and I will tell you;" he put his hand into his pocket, pulled out something, and was putting it down; I said, "That won't do for me;" I caught his hand, and found three shillings in it.
Larkin's Defence. I accused the prosecutor of robbing me of a sovereign; Treadaway said, "It is in his cart" - he was going to get into his cart and Treadaway struck him - I had two sovereigns and two or three shillings in my pocket, and one sovereign was there when I was taken; the prosecutor was intoxicated.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18320517-151]

1278. STEPHEN LEWER was indicted for stealing, on the 2nd of April, 410 agate drops, value 10l., and 308 pieces of agate, value 12l. the goods of John Cath, his master; and JOHN LEWER was indicted for feloniously receiving the same, well knowing them to have been stolen.
MR. LEE conducted the prosecution.
JOHN CATH. I am a warehouseman, and live in St. Mary-axe. In April I had some rough agates consigned to me from France - Stephen Lewer, was in my employ as porter; I received information, and went, between the 2nd and 9th of April, to Mr. Couch, at Clerkenwell, in consequence of which I had the prisoners taken - they both went before the Lord Mayor, and I believe what they said was taken down.
GEORGE ALVARES. I was in the prosecutor's employ. He had a box of rough agates in his warehouse, but I did not see them when they were brought there; I had occasion, in March, to look into that box - the contents were not the same as they had been before; Stephen Lewer was then in my master's employ - I told him he had been at these agates, and asked him to tell me what he had done with them; he said he knew nothing about them.
GEORGE COUCH. I am a lapidary, and live on Clerkenwell-green. John Lewer came to my shop, I think it was sometime in May, about three weeks before he was committed; he offered me two pieces of white agate, and said he had been recommended to me by a person in Houndsditch, named Horne, with whom I had done some business; John Lewer said there was a quantity more of these agates, and what he brought was a mere sample - I said it was an article I was rather in want of, as there were none in the market at that time - he said he would let me see the bulk, and there was a box of them which belonged to another person; I bought the two first, and he called again, I think three times - I bought of him to the amount of 3l. or 4l., as far as my memory will serve me.
DANIEL FORRESTER. I am an officer. I apprehended Stephen Lewer - he denied all knowledge of the fact; I took John Lewer afterwards, and produced to him a sample of the same description of property, and asked him if he knew a person named Carter; he said, "What! has he been saying all about it?" or words to that effect - he then told me he had taken some of these stones, and that Mr. Couch was in want of some of the white ones but not the yellow; he said he had not any of the black ones, but the white drops and stones he had disposed of to Mr. Couch, and he had them from his brother Stephen - he gave another account of them at the Mansion-house, but that was taken down, I suppose; he then said the person said it was no object to him how he became possessed of them, but he gave him 4s. or 5s. for selling them - that he told Mr. Couch he had them from on board a ship; that he had received about 7l. for them, and had sold none at any other place.
MR FRANCIS HOBLER. I am clerk to the Lord Mayor. I recollect the two prisoners being brought before the Lord Mayor on this charge on the 6th of April - they were examined, and the substance of what they said was taken down - (reads).
John said, "I sold to the amount of 6l. or 7l. to him, and none to any one else," alluding to Couch, who had just spoken; he then told where he worked - and that he had been ill; Stephen said, "I took them at different times;" Mr. Cath said, "I cannot swear to any of them."
I have no remembrance of the case; on the first examination Stephen only was in custody - that was on the 2nd of April; there was no statement then made; Mr. Cath said he wanted time, and the parties were remanded till the Friday following, and then that passed which I have read -Mr. Cath made a charge of having lost some agates out of his warehouse; I cannot undertake to state that I took down all that passed - it was not read over to the prisoners.
COURT. Q. May you have omitted something that the prisoners said? A. I cannot say that I wrote down every word but the substance; I might have omitted some things, but these words were used.
MR. CATH. I was before the Lord Mayor when these men were charged with the felony; I recollect the Lord Mayor asked Stephen where he got the agates which he gave to his brother - he said he had stolen them at different times from my warehouse, (or most likely he said took them,) and given them to his brother; the question was asked of John where he got the agates from, and he replied from his brother Stephen, and he was to have a few shillings commission for selling them - John knew that Stephen was in my employ; I believe John is a wine-porter - he was not likely to be in possession of articles of this description on his own account.
John Lewer. I did not know that my brother was at work for you. Witness. I cannot tell that he was aware of it, but I think it most likely - he had lived a year and a half with me.
DANIEL FORRESTER. John Lewer was set at liberty, and he attended again of his own accord.
JURY to MR. COUCH. Q. What sort of articles, and how many of each did you purchase? A. I think twelve pieces of rough agate, about thirty pairs of drops, and twenty or thirty oval cut ones; I retail the largest of them at 1s. a pair - I retail them generally at about 9d., and I gave 6d. - the amount of all I bought was 3l. 1s. 7d.
S. LEWER - GUILTY. Aged 19.
J. LEWER - GUILTY. Aged 22.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18320517-153]
1279. JOHN INGLEY was indicted for stealing, on the 18th of April, 1 watch, value 13l., the goods of David Hecksher, in the dwelling-house of Sarah Benjamin .
DAVID HECKSHER. I lodge in St. James'-place, Aldgate; the prisoner lived servant in that house. I lost a gold lever watch on the 18th of April, from my bed room; it was worth 13l.
JOHN DELBRIDGE. I am shopman to Mr. Cording, a pawnbroker, in Aldgate. The prisoner came there on the night of the 18th of April, and brought this watch, on which I lent 7l. 10s.; he gave the name of John Aaron, No. 16, Essex Street - I am sure he is the man; he said it hadbelonged to his father, who was lately dead; and that he had been unfortunate in life.
Prisoner. Q.Look at me; I never saw you till I was at the office? A. I am positive you are the man, you were taken two days after; I gave you seven sovereigns, and the rest in silver.
COURT. Q. How far is your shop from where the prosecutor lives? A.About a quarter of a mile; it was pawned between eight and and nine o'clock in the evening - he was in the shop nearly a quarter of an hour, I had several people there; I asked if he was a lodger, and put L. for lodger on the duplicate.
JURY. Q. Was he in the shop? A. Yes, and close to a gas - he had not the same dress on then; I had not seen him before, but I am certain of him - I described him to the prosecutor.
MR. HECKSHER. I took him myself; there was no officer employed.
COURT. Q. Are there any other lodgers in the house? A. No, there is one female servant; it is Miss Benjamin's house - her brother and sister live with her - the female servant attended to my room - the prisoner might have seen I had a watch in my room as he went up stairs; I am a watch-maker, and have a great many watches there - there were four together - I missed one; I took the prisoner four days afterwards; I missed the watch at eleven o'clock at night - it was from Mr. Delbridge's description I took the prisoner; he had gone away that night and we saw no more of him till he was taken.
Prisoner. Q. Had not I given notice to go away? A. I believe he had; I missed the watch on the 18th, and can be on my oath I had it in my hand at one o'clock - he left about two in the afternoon.
JOSEPH BENJAMIN. I live with my sister. The prisoner lived there, and left about two o'clock on the 18th of April; he said he would go, and asked for three days wages, which I paid him - he had lived with us seventeen years off and on, and behaved honest; we had some dispute that day about his being gone two or three hours to Billingsgate; he did not return till near two o'clock, and then left; he took some aprons and other things with him - I did not see him go to his room to fetch them, the maid is not here; the prisoner went up and got his clothes; the prosecutor's door is generally closed - he works at home in general.
MR. HECKSHER. I had been called out in a hurry; I shut the door but did not lock it.
JURY. Q. Is there no one else in the house? A. There is a boy, but he had no business up stairs; he could go up.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18320517-154]
1280. MARY CONNELLY was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of April, 1 blanket, value 10s., the goods of Edmund Bird.
JOHANNA BIRD. I am the wife of Edmund Bird, who keeps the George, in West-street, Smithfield . On the 14th of April I saw the prisoner leave our house with a large blanket in an apron - I sent one of the waiters after her; the blanket had been hanging in the passage to dry, and I saw it safe when she came in five minutes before.
THOMAS PROTHERO. I am an officer. I took the prisoner on Monday the 16th of April - I have not found the blanket.
JAMES BROWN. I was called by my mistress to go after the prisoner - I had been in the tap-room, when she came in; she had no blanket then - I gave her a light; I was up stairs, and my mistress called to me that she had gone out with a blanket; I went out, but could not find her.
Prisoner's Defence. A friend told me to call on them there, between seven and eight o'clock - I went, but could not see them; I then went out, and on the Monday morning the officer came and asked if I had been to the house, and if I had taken a blanket - I said I had been there, but I had taken nothing out; I saw no blanket, and if I had been seen to come out with it they should have stopped me.
THOMAS PROTHERO. I never asked whether she took the blanket, or whether she had been at the house - I took her there, and Mrs. Bird indentified her.
JURY to MRS. BIRD. Q. Was the prisoner a customer? A. Yes; she was well known in the place; I called after her, but she turned up a court and got away before the waiter could get after her - she had come in empty handed.
GUILTY. Aged 43. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18320517-155]
1281. MARY McBRIDE was indicted for stealing, on the 21st of April, 2 shifts, value 6s., the goods of Samuel Michael.
JAMES TAGUS SHOUT. I am shopman to a pawnbroker. On the 21st of April I took in these articles of the prisoner- I advanced 1s. 6d. on them.
SAMUEL MICHAEL. I live in Fetter-lane. I employed the prisoner to clean the house - she lived in the kitchen; I know these two shifts - I bought them myself because my wife was in the hospital, and she would not use them because they had marks on them - they were clean in the box; I did not employ the prisoner to pawn them - I missed them on the 23rd of April; I bought them myself in the fair in Petticoat-lane, six or seven weeks ago - I know one was marked, but I do not know what it was; I cannot read - I know them by these stripes.
Prisoner's Defence. I worked for him six weeks - one of these shifts is mine; I said to him, on the Saturday night, "Mr. Michael, I will be obliged to you to let me have the price of a pair of shoes;" he said he had no change, and I took one of my own shifts, and the other one which might belong to him, but I found it in the room -I did not mean to defraud him of it; I told him I must have something to pawn.
SAMUEL MICHAEL. She did not tell me so, nor ask for any money; I owed her nothing - she had drawn more than was due to her; she did not tell me she had pawned any thing.
Prisoner. He is single - the girl he keeps is an English girl. Witness. I was married at Hull, about three years ago; I do not know at what church, nor who was present - I had known my wife before; I travel with goods as a furrier.
COURT. Q. What did the prisoner do for you? A. She cleaned the room and lighted the fire; she lives in the kitchen with another woman, and I live on the first floor.
DIANA LEVY. I live at Hackney, and am a furrier. -The prosecutor employed me - on the day he charged the prisoner with this I was there, and she desired I would search her; I found three duplicates in her pocket - I read them to him; one was for a shirt, one for a cloak, and one for these two shifts - she told me that she had her victuals and drink, and 1s. a week - she then said he had given her a bed, which was worth 3s., and the whole of the time she was with him was but three weeks, and she had drawn another 1s. - when I found this duplicate she said that one of the shifts was the prosecutor's, and one was her own; she did not tell him, in my presence, that if she had not money she would pawn something - I saw 10s. taken from her; I do not know Mrs. Michael; I had been there but a fortnight.
WILLIAM HENRY KING. I am a constable. I received the prisoner in charge, and got the duplicates from Levy.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18320517-156]
1282. CHARLES BRIGHTLEY, JOSEPH JACKSON, and WILLIAM SMITH were indicted for stealing, on the 12th of May, 1 handkerchief, value 2s., the goods of George Mence Boyes, from his person.
GEORGE MENCE BOYES. I am a merchant's clerk. On the 12th of May, in the afternoon, I was passing under an arch-way, in Upper Thames-street, and felt a twitch at my pocket; I turned, and missed my handkerchief - Brightley and Smith were close to me; I seized Brightley, and my handkerchief fell from under his coat - I pulled him a short distance; the officer ran up, caught Smith, and told a person to take Jackson, as they had been together - Jackson was walking off, but was brought back.
JAMES BATES. I am a draper. I was speaking to the officer, and he drew my attention to the three prisoners; I saw them go to a stall, buy an orange, and then walk on towards Billingsgate; they met the prosecutor -I saw Brightley touch his pocket; they all turned, and followed him, and two or three doors before they came to Fish-street-hill, they made another attempt; he then went on under the arch, and Brightley there took the handkerchief - the others were close to him at the time; he dropped the handkerchief - I took Jackson, who was walking off, and the officer took Smith.
PHILIP HENRY PARISH. I am an officer. I saw the three prisoners, and pointed them out to Bates; I followed with him about half way towards Billingsgate, where they met the prosecutor, and returned after him -I did not see the attempt made, but I saw he had hold of Brightley; I went up, and the prisoners were secured.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Brightley's Defence. I was waiting at the London-docks - a gentleman asked me to carry a box to the steam-pocket, which I did, and he gave me 6d. for it - as I came back this gentleman took me.
Jackson's Defence. I was going on an errand, when the prosecutor laid hold of Brightley, and then the other gentleman said I was one of them.
Smith's Defence. My father sent me to my aunt's, in the Strand, and told me to call on a man at Billingsgate; I forgot it till I got to the bridge - I turned back; I saw the man, and was then going on, and they took me behind a cart.
JAMES BATES. I am sure he was with the others when the attempts were made, and the handkerchief drawn.
BRIGHTEY - GUILTY. Aged 19.
Transported for Seven Years.
JACKSON - GUILTY. Aged 20.
SMITH - GUILTY. Aged 19.
Confined Six Months.

1283. JOHN TUDOR was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of April, 1 handkerchief, value 2s., the goods of John Robert Witherden, from his person.
RICHARD BOTHEROYD KAY. I keep the King's Arms, in Beech-street, Barbican. On the night of the 13th of April, I saw Mr. Witherden pass my door, with a handkerchief hanging nearly out of his pocket - I saw the prisoner and another, rather bigger, following the prosecutor; I followed, till they came to Messrs. Hoare's, the bankers, in Fleet-street - I saw the prisoner take the handkerchief from the prosecutor; I collared and took him to the watch-house.
JOHN ROBERT WITHERDEN. I live in Bacon-street, Bethnal-green, and was in Fleet-street when the witness spoke to me; I had gone through Beech-street - the witness produced to me the handkerchief, which I had had in my pocket - the prisoner was then in custody.
JOHN READ. I am an officer, and took the prisoner.(Property produced and sworn to.)
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 14. - Fined 1s. and Discharged.

1284. CHARLES KELLY was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of December, 600 sets of printed books, value 100l., the goods of Henry Colburn.
THREE OTHER COUNTS, varying the description of the property.
WILLIAM MOSS. I am a printer; and in December, 1830, I was in the employ of Mr. Baylis and his partner; I missed 600 sets of printed books from their premises; they were tied up, and ready to go to the publishers.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. When had you seen them safe? A. On the 9th of July, 1830.
JOHN CHIDLEY. I live in Goswell-street, and am a bookseller. In January, 1831, I bought a quantity of printed paper of the prisoner - he said he had bought it at Mr. Colburn's, and there was about half a ton of it - I asked why he did not bring the whole, or let one of my men go and get it - he said the men were busy, and he could but bring it by 1 cwt. at a time; I agreed to have it so - I did not see him again till last April, when I saw him in Barbican; I thought he was the man, and as soon as he saw me, he ran off, but was bindered - I got up to him, took him, and said "You must go with me;" he said, "I will see you d-d first;" he gave a cast with his arm, and got from me - I seized him again, and kept him down till the officer took him - this is the paper - I had no other like it.
WILLIAM MOSS. I cannot swear that this had not been sent to Mr. Colburn, and he is not here - I had not seen it from July, 1830.
NOT GUILTY.

Second Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.

1286. ANN WOOD was indicted for stealing, on the 21st of April, 7 sovereigns, the monies of Richard Parker, from his person.
GEORGE KEMP (Police-constable N 82). On Saturday night, the 21st of April, I was in Great Cambridge-street, Hackney-road; I saw the prisoner and prosecutor in conversation together - I saw the prisoner leave him; I crossed the road, and asked if he had been robbed - in consequence of what he said I followed, and took the prisoner - she dropped three sovereigns; I found two at her feet, and one was rolling away; I took her to the prosecutor, and then to the station-house; the prosecutor was in liquor.
Cross-examined by MR. CLARKSON. Q. Were there any other women about? A. There was one following behind them, on the other side of the way: I had seen the prisoner, but had no conversation with her; I did not tell the Magistrate that I went on a little way, and picked something up; I said I found two sovereigns on the ground, and one was rolling - the prosecutor had been drinking, but knew what he was about, and could walk.
RICHARD PARKER. I live in Parker-street, Kingsland-road, and am a surveyor. On the evening of the 21st of April I was in Great Cambridge-street, Hackney-road -I had been drinking a little, but was able to walk, and do business; it was between eight and nine o'clock - I saw the prisoner; she followed, and came up to me in York-street; we conversed together for some minutes - the moment she left me I put my hand to my pocket, and missed my money; I saw the Policeman that instant, and whether he spoke to me first or I to him I cannot say - I had had seven sovereigns loose in my right-hand trousers pocket; I had them with my hand in my pocket when she joined me - no other person had been near enough to take it from the time I felt it safe.
Cross-examined. Q.Then you think you was very little the worse for liquor? A. Yes; when she met me I had my hand and my money in my pocket - she must have taken it from my pocket; I was aware of it when I had lost it; my hand was not in my pocket all the time -I had counted my sovereigns before she came into my company; I had been at a public-house in the Commercial-road - I was not drunk; I swear no other person had been in my company - I cannot say whether I had buttoned my pocket when I took my hand out; I had made no contract with the prisoner; I first met her in York-street, two streets from Great Cambridge-street, I suppose a quarter of a mile from my house - she had hold of my arm for the purpose of getting my money; I swear I gave her nothing when I took my hand out of my pocket.
GUILTY. Aged 27. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18320517-161]
1287. THOMAS GASCOYNE, JOHN JONES, and JOHN HUTCHINS were indicted for stealing, on the 15th of May, 1 purse, value 1s.; 2 sovereigns, 2 half-crowns, 6 shillings, and 5 sixpences, the property of Brook Brydges Parbly, Esq., from his person.
THOMAS MADDEN. I am a constable of Kensington. On the 15th of May I was in the garden belonging to the King's Head, Kensington; there were a number of persons assembled at a meeting - I saw all the prisoners there, and watched them; I saw Colonel Parbly - Gascoyne, the prisoner, extended his right hand to the prosecutor's right-hand trousers pocket; I did not observe where the prosecutor's hand was at the time, but I heard Jones make some remark to Gascoyne, who replied,"I can't get his manler off," which means a hand; Jones then attempted his pocket in the same way - some more words passed between them, and Gascoyne said, "Why don't you get his d-d mauler off?" I then saw Hutchins come from the rear in front of the colonel; he looked up in his face, and said Mr. Hume (who had just spoken) was a very clever fellow, and he added, "Before Mr. Hume leaves the hustings, let us give him three cheers"- when they were given Hutchins said, "Let us give him one cheer more;" when the fourth cheer was given the colouel took his hat off, and waved it as well as he could in the crowd, raising his right arm; and either Gascoyne or Jones was supporting his arm up; the three prisoners left the crowd immediately the fourth cheer was given - I asked the colonel if he had lost any thing; he felt in his pocket, and said, "I have lost my purse" - I ran through the crowd, and succeeded in stopping the three prisoners, with the assistance of some other persons, just as they were going out of the door - Hutchins had his right hand in his pocket; I examined his pocket, and found in it this purse, with two sovereigns and 13s. 6d. in it - he claimed it; I took them to the station, and asked the colonel to describe his purse - he claimed it, and said there were two sovereigns in it, and some silver, but he could not exactly say what - I found some money on Jones, and on Gascoyne - I found on Hutchins, besides the purse, a half-sovereign, 9s. 6d. in silver, and I think 2d. in copper, and a pocket-book.
Cross-examined by MR. CLARKSON. Q.What did you find on Gascoyne? A.2s. 6d. in silver, and 1 1/2d. - there was a great crowd, and the colonel was in the midst of it; there was no difficulty in distinguishing the prisoners - I did not go to the Reform Meeting as an officer, but if I had seen a breach of the peace I should have interfered; I was not quite certain when the attempt was made, and if I had attempted to take them then I should have had very little chance in the crowd - I was not certain that the colonel was robbed till I saw them leaving the crowd - I did not see Jones or Gascoyne speak to Hutchins; there might be one or two persons between him and the colonel when these attempts were made.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. You went partly as a reformer yourself? A. You may take it so if you please, but of course I was an officer - I applauded the speeches, but I never lifted my hand; I think the prisoners were more anxious to see others cheer than to cheer themselves - Hutchins claimed thepurse to the last; I do not know whether it is a common purse, I have seen many like it.
BROOK BRYDGES PARBLY. I am a colonel in the East India Company's service. I was at the King's Arms garden, attending the meeting, as I live in the parish, in Brompton-square; Lord Teynham was in the chair - I had a purse of this sort and colour, with two sovereigns and some loose silver in it - I missed it when the officer spoke to me; I believe this is my purse - I recollect the cheers being proposed; I had before that had my hand on my side, but when Mr. Hume was leaving the meeting, I lifted my hand, and took off my hat - by that means my pocket was exposed.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Was your coat buttoned? A. Yes; I cannot swear to the purse - Hutchins insisted that it was his from the first; there was a great crowd.
Cross-examined by MR. CLARKSON. Q. Did you observe any communication between the prisoners? A. No- I could not swear to any of them.
JURY to THOMAS MADDEN. Q. Did the prisoners go away together? A. They were close together when they quitted, and went at the same time.
Gascoyne. I was not near the door; I was in the crowd - another person took me. Witness. I took him and Hutchins near the door; there was some crowd there - I called a person to assist me; I called to a special-constable to take Jones - they were all going away together: there were several speeches after Mr. Hume left.
Hutchins. The purse is mine - I have had it three or four months; I bought in the Strand, where you can buy fifty every day - this officer did not take it from me; another person took it out of my pocket, and gave it to him- I said it was mine, and that it had 2l. 13s. 6d. in it. -Witness. He had his hand in his pocket, grasping the purse, and I took it; I said, "If it is yours, you can tell what is in it - how many sovereigns are there?" he said three; I said, "How much silver?" he said about 14s. - he guessed the silver right within 6d.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Are you sure he did not say two sovereigns? A. He said two, after he saw the money counted, but at first he said three.
COLONEL PARBLY. The gold was at one end, and the silver at the other.
GASCOYNE - GUILTY. Aged 28.
HUTCHINS - GUILTY. Aged 36.
JONES - GUILTY. Aged 34.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18320517-162]
1288. JULIUS PICARD was indicted for stealing, on the 3rd of April, 1 guitar, value 4l., the goods of Angelo Bernedetto Ventura.
The prisoner being a native of France, had the evidence explained by an interpreter.
ANGELO BERNEDETTO VENTURA. I live in Cirencester-place, and am a professor of music. Three or four days before the beginning of last month I became acquainted with the prisoner at the appartments of Mr. Burger, when he invited me to take a little play - he applied to me to lend him a guitar, which I did several times; at the beginning of April he asked me to lend him one for a night- he was not to pay for it; I did not want him to take it then, as I wanted to clean it, but he took it from a table; he was to return it the day afterwards - two days afterwards he invited me to go to his house in Bath-place; he came in a horse and gig; Mr. Crutz came with him; he saw a very beautiful guitar at my house, worth ten guineas- he said he wished to show it to his Baroness, as she would want to take a lesson very likely, and he invited me to go to Bath-place; I went there to tea - he gave his address " Baron Dillon, No. 13, Bath-place, New-road;" I went, and took another guitar with me; he wished me to fetch my elegant guitar; two or three days afterwards I found the guitar I had first lent him at a pawnbroker's, in St. Martin's-lane, and two other instruments - the prisoner came to me that day; I told him I had found where he had pawned my instruments, and if he did not return them I would have him arrested; he then gave me up the duplicates of these two guitars, and a harp - this is the one I first lent him.
Cross-examined by MR. ADOLPHUS. Q. You teach music, and deal in musical instruments? A. Yes; he had all three of the guitars in his possession - I leat him one, and these other two were sold; I had not sold him the three, to he paid for in three months; I had not asked him to return the one I left him - I went to a great many pawnbrokers, because I suspected I had got with a set of swindlers; I did stand once where the prisoner now does, but I went out with very great honour; you scolded my wife very much - I was charged with bigamy.
WILLIAM WILLIAMS. I am shopman to a pawnbroker in St. Martin's-lane. I have a guitar, which was pawned for 1l. 10s., but not by the prisoner - this is the duplicate.
PROSECUTOR. This is one he gave me.
Prisoner's Defence. He sold me the three instruments before two witnesses.
GUILTY. - Aged 49. - Judgment Respited.
[trialAccount t18320517-163]
1289. JOHN MARTIN was indicted for stealing, on the 2nd of May, 1 handkerchief, value 2s, 6d., the goods of James Cumming, from his person.
JAMES CUMMING. I am a surgeon, and live at Tavistock-place. On the evening of the 2nd of May I was in Upper King-street, Bloomsbury; I heard a cry of Stop thief! missed my handkerchief, and saw the prisoner pursued by Mr. Rix - he was secured with my handkerchief in his possession, which he said I had dropped.
WILLIAM RIX. I am a plumber. I was in Upper King-street, and saw the prisoner and another person; I saw the handkerchief taken from the pocket of a gentleman, who, I believe, was the prosecutor, the prisoner was within a yard of the person who took it; and the other person gave it to the prisoner, who put it into his breast pocket - I cried Stop thief! and the prisoner ran into a mews, I took him there; I had lost sight of him, but I know he is the person.
WILLIAM JAMES. (Police-constable E 17). I received prisoner, and found this handkerchief on him.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 19. - Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18320517-164]
1290. EDMUND WEBSTER was indicted for stealing, on the 30th of April, 1 copper, value 25s.; and 1 copper pot, value 15s., the goods of Sarah Waterfield; and fixed to a building; against the Statute.
JOHN CALLER (Police-constable S 67). On the morning of the 30th of April, about half-past six o'clock, I saw the prisoner in Pancras-road, carrying this copper and pot in a bag - I asked if he had not got a copper; he said Yes, that he had found these articles in a field, and was going to take them to the station-house; I said he had better go to the station with me - he said he had no objection, but when he had got a few steps, he put them down and said I might take them; I took them, but was forced to put them down once or twice to take hold of him, as he was very obstinate; I went to the Adam and Eve, in Pancras-road, and found the wash-house door had been broken open, and this chisel was there, which exactly corresponded with the marks on the door; the copper was missed, but the bricks had been pulled down, and we could not fit it.
SARAH WATERFIELD. I am a widow, and keep the Adam and Eve . The officer called me up - I missed a copper and a pot; I am sure these are mine; I had seen them safe on the Saturday night, and this was on the Monday morning.
Prisoner's Defence. I was in search of work, and saw these articles in a bag; I was going to take them to the station-house.
GUILTY. Aged 22. - Transported for Seven Years.

1291. ROBERT WILLIAMS was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of May, 3 chisels, value 1s.; 1 pair of compasses, value 6d.; 1 gouge, value 6d.; 2 gimblets, value 6d., and 3 files, value 6d., the goods of Henry Bull; 4 chisels, value 2s., and 2 gimblets, value 8d., the goods of John Syrett.
THOMAS WILSON (Police-constable T 59). On the night of the 1st of May I was on duty at Hyde-park-terrace, about eight o'clock; I went to the back of the buildings; I then went by a ladder into an attic of an unfurnished house, and found the prisoner there, with these articles on him - I asked how he came there; he made no answer.
HENRY BULL. I am a carpenter, and was at work at these houses. On the morning of the 2nd of May, when I went to work, I missed some tools, which I had left there; some of these are mine, and some belong to John Syrett.
GUILTY. Aged 20. - Transported for Seven Years.

1292. THOMAS ATKINS was indicted for stealing, on the 5th of May, 1 umbrella, value 8s., and 1 hat, value 5s., the goods of Richard Molineux.
JOHN TODD. I am an ironmonger, and live in Bury-street, Bloomsbury. On the 5th of May I met the prisoner in Bury-street, with an umbrella and a hat -I stopped him - Mr. Molineux came up and claimed them; he was taken into custody - he wanted me to go up the street, and said he would point out the person who gave them to him.
RICHARD MOLINEUX. I am a coach-wheelwright, and live in Little Russell-street; I came up while Mr. Todd had hold of the prisoner, who was scuffling to get away -I knew the hat and umbrella immediately, and gave him in charge; I had seen them in my passage half an hour before - my house is two hundred yards from where he was taken.
JOHN CARNEY. (Police-constable E 34). I received the prisoner; he said a man gave these things to him, but did not say where he was to take them.
GUILTY. Aged 24. - Transported for Seven Years.

1293. WILLIAM ANDREWS was indicted for stealing, on the 11th of April, 1 saw, value 3s., and 1 knife, value 8s., the goods of Isaac Clift.
ISAAC CLIFT. I live in Holywell-lane, Shoreditch, and am a patten-manufacturer. I employed the prisoner on the 10th of April; when the men left work, I missed a saw from the shop - the prisoner came to work on the 11th, and I went into the shop, and asked him and the rest of the men if they knew any thing of it; the prisoner said he had taken it to a man where he lodged, and would go and get it - I missed my knife that day, and from information I found them in pledge; the prisoner absconded the day I missed my knife; he came on the following Friday, and I had him taken - he begged my pardon, and said he would make any arrangement, if I would not prosecute him.
THOMAS STEVENS. I am a fellow-workman of the prisoner's. On the 10th of April I met him in Shoreditch - he told me had taken the saw, and pawned it for 6d.; on the Wednesday I heard him in the passage - he said,"Larry, bring the knife up, I want to take it to be ground."
JAMES ASHLEY. I am apprentice to a pawnbroker, in Shoreditch. I have a saw, pawned by the prisoner, for 1s. on the 10th of April.
JOHN NEALE. I am assistant to a pawnbroker. I have a patten-knife, pawned on the 11th of April, to the best of my belief, by the prisoner - I know I have seen him there.
The prisoner put in a written Defence, stating that he could not get his wages, and had pledged the saw to obtain victuals.
ISAAC CLIFT. I did not owe him any thing.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18320517-168]
1294. HENRY BROUGHTON was indicted for stealing, on the 26th of April, 1 coat, value 7s., the property of John Morgan.
PHILIP JONES (Police-constable N 217). On the 26th of April I was at my window in Church-street, Hackney - I saw the prisoner coming down the grove with a great coat; two more men were behind him - he was walking very fast, and when he turned into the main road, he ran as fast as he could; I ran after him for about ten minutes, and took him - while he was running he threw the coat down in the dirt; this is it - he said at the station that he bought it of the two men who were with him, for 2s. - the other two had run off; it was a quarter before eight o'clock in the morning, and he was coming from the prosecutor's.
JOHN MORGAN. I am in the employ of Mr. Barber, of Hackney; I attend his stage-coaches. On the 26th of April I placed my great coat in a chaise, in his coach-house, about seven o'clock in the morning - I missed it at eight, and saw it afterwards in possession of the officer.(Property produced and sworn to.)
The prisoner put in a written Defence, stating that he had bought the coat of two men in Hackney-fields.
GUILTY. Aged 22. - Transported for Seven Years.

1295. CATHERINE BROOKS was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of April, 1 pair of trousers, value 10s., the goods of Thomas Stafford and John Stafford.
JAMES TRAIL. I am in the employ of Thomas and John Stafford, pawnbrokers, St. John-street. On the evening of the 12th of April, the prisoner came into the shop with another woman to pawn a shawl - while this was going on, the prisoner went to the door, and looked at a pair of trousers which were tied to an iron bar, and I suppose she untied them - she then came back to the counter - went out, and came in again - then went out and took the trousers; I went and found her with them two doors off; I think she was intoxicated.
WILLIAM CLEMENTS (Police-constable G 82). I took the prisoner; she said the woman who was with her took them down, gave them to her, and desired her to run off with them.
GUILTY. Aged 37.

1296. GEORGE BRIANT was indicted for stealing' on the 29th of February, 1 saw, value 3s., the goods of Thomas Keen; 1 saw, value 8s., the goods of Thomas Clapton; and 1 apron, value 6d., the goods of James Harwin.
THOMAS JOHNSON (Police-constable G 81). I took the prisoner and another man into custody on a charge of common assault; I found on the prisoner twenty-four duplicates, in a purse - some of them relate to the tools in question; I have found three persons who claim some of them - this apron I found on him also.
JOHN DAVIS. I am servant to Mr. Cordwell, a pawnbroker, in Exmouth-street. I have three saws, two ploughs, and other articles, pawned at his shop - these are the duplicates of them.
GEORGE FRY. I am in the service of Mr. Lorton, a pawnbroker. I have two saws, pawned on the 29th of April - this is the duplicate, but I did not take them in.
THOMAS KEEN. This saw is mine - I lost it on the 29th of February, and this other saw is Thomas Clapton 's - I went with the officer to take the prisoner, and found on him this apron, which belongs to another workman; there were several other saws and tools lost.
JAMES HARWIN. This is my apron, and was in the same shop as the tools were.
Prisoner's Defence. I bought the apron in Oxford-street - they have all sworn false against me; I found the duplicates - I put them into a cupboard in my room.
GUILTY. Aged 38. - Transported for Seven Years

1297. JOHN BALDWIN was indicted for stealing, on the 4th of April, 1 waistcoat, value 14s., the goods of John Foster.
JOHN FOSTER. I live in Queen-street, Edgware-road. I had seen the prisoner at my son-in-law's, and asked him to assist me to move a few things - I then missed my waistcoat.
HENRY RIDER. I am a pawnbroker, and live at Chelsea. I took in this waistcoat from the prisoner on the 2nd of April, and lent 3s. on it; I am certain of his person.

1298. BENJAMIN BREWER was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of May, 2 trusses of hay, value 5s., and 3 bushels of oats and chaff, mixed together, value 4s., the goods of Thomas Cobley, the younger.
THOMAS COBLEY, JUN. I am a carman, and live in Essex-street, Whitechapel. I had a cart in my yard, loaded with dung, on the 1st of May; it was just going to start -I received information, and examined it; I found under the tilt two trusses of hay, similar to that which I had in my loft - it was the prisoner's business to drive that cart; I spoke to him about it - he said he got it on the road, and then said he did not know how it came there - I sent for an officer, and took him; after he was gone to the station a sack of oats and chaff was found in the cart.
JOHN KEMBER. I am foreman to the prosecutor. On the 1st of May the cart of dung was there, and two trusses of hay and a sack of oats and chaff mixed together, were found in it; I had been in the loft, and counted the trusses of clover hay - I then went to breakfast; the prisoner was loading the dung - when I came back he was still doing the same; I went into the loft and counted the hay again, and missed two trusses - I know he had not hay in his cart when he came to the yard; the two trusses were found on the copse in front of the cart - they were of the same sort as those in the loft, and made up the forty-four which I had at first counted - he first said he found it on the road, and then that he did not know how it got there; we had some mixed oats and chaff in the loft, but we could not miss any of them - that found in his cart corresponds with it exactly, and the sack, in which they were found seemed to be one which he had brought empty.
Prisoner's Defence. I went to get a pint of beer; I do not know who put them there.
GUILTY. Aged 30.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18320517-173]
1299. WILLIAM BULL was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of April, 1 carpet, value 18s., the goods of William Clack.
SARAH CLACK. I am the wife of William Clack, a baker, who lives at Hertford. I came to town, and on the 10th of April I was going from Shoreditch to Bethnal -green-road - I had a carpet with me with a string attached to it; the prisoner came up, and caught hold of the parcel- he let it go; he caught it a second time, and tugged at it till he broke the string, and made away with it; he ran down Wentworth-street, and turned to the left - I am quite positive he is the person; I observed his features very particularly - he had a dark coat, a shabby black hat, and corderoy trousers on; there was a person brought up to me afterwards, who was not the man - I saw the prisoner in custody on the 21st, and knew him; I gave 18s. 6d. for the carpet - Mary Crawley was with me; I have not seen it since - a number of people came about me, but gave me no assistance; I went to a public-house, at the corner, and applied to the landlord, but he gave me no assistance; the officer then came - I gave him information, and describedthe person who had robbed me; when I had passed Shoreditch church it was a quarter before twelve o'clock, and about five minutes after I was robbed, the officer took out his watch, and said it was half-past twelve.
Cross-examined by MR. LEE. Q. Is that a place of great thoroughfare? A. I should consider so - it must have taken me more than half an hour to go there from Shoreditch church - I was a good deal agitated, but I am not deceived in the person; I did not say before the Magistrate that the man had a light jacket on - I always said it was a dark coat; another person was taken from my description, but that was not the person.
MARY CRAWLEY. I am sister to Mrs. Clarck. I was with her in Brick-lane - I am positive the prisoner is the person who took the parcel; my sister appealed to the people about to assist, but nobody did; I saw the prisoner's face - he had a dark coat on, a shabby black hat, and corderoy trousers.
Cross-examined. Q.I suppose you were a good deal frightened? A. Yes, but I noticed the person of the prisoner; I did not see him again for eleven days - I am sure my sister did not say he had a light jacket on, or a light coat.
MARY ANN FOSTER. I am the wife of John Foster - we live in London-street, London-road; the prisoner's father and mother occupy my front parlour - they have lodged with me four months; the prisoner lived with them. I recollect, on the 10th of April, I went into their room for rent - I saw the prisoner and his mother there; she was ill in bed, and was mending his trousers, which I believe were corderoy or fustian, and he had a short jacket on, either fustain or corderoy - it was a dark colour; my house is a mile and a half from Wentworth-street; as near as I can judge it was twelve o'clock, for directly I left I went to get my little boy from school; I left the prisoner in the room, waiting for his trousers.
SAMUEL PRENDERGRASS. I am an officer of Lambeth-street. On the 10th of April I was in Old Montague-street, opposite to Wentworth-street - I went to a public-house, and saw the prosecutrix there, complaining of her loss; she was much agitated - I asked her particularly the description of the person, in consequence of which I apprehended a man in five or six minutes afterwards; she said he was not the man - she then described the prisoner; I looked at my watch when I had been two or three minutes in the house - it was half-past twelve o'clock, but I afterwards found my watch was a quarter of an hour too slow.
Cross-examined. Q. Did you make an entry of the charge in the book? A. Yes - she said he had a fustian or a light jacket on, that he was about five feet four inches high, and of a pale complexion.
JAMES JOHN CONNOLEY (Police-constable H 135). I received information on the 13th of April, and on the 14th I saw the prisoner coming out of Wentworth-street with two others - I followed them; the prisoner was going to turn into a passage on the right-hand side, towards Whitechapel, but he saw me, left his companious, and crossed the road; I went to him, and said, "I think I want you, I am not certain - will you go with me to Bethnal-green-road?" he asked what I wanted with him - I said that was not a usual thing to state there, because if he was not the person it would be generally circulated; he then went with me to No. 51, Bethnal-green-road, where Mary Crawley lives - she came down stairs, and before she got into the shop, she said, "That is the man that robbed my sister;" he said,"You must be mistaken;" I took him to the station, booked the charge, and he was sent to the office - he was remanded till the following Saturday, when Mrs. Clack attended - there were sixteen or twenty persons together, but she picked him out from the others; she said she was quite certain of him by his face.
Prisoner's Defence. I was not nearer to Whitechapel on that day than London-bridge; I then went to Euston-square, to see for my uncle; I did not leave my father till past one o'clock.
MARY ANN COLLINS. I live in the same house with the prisoner. On the 10th of April I met the prisoner and his father in the London-road, about half-past twelve o'clock; I was going to the public-house for my dinner beer when my father came home - there is a clock at the public-house.
COURT. Q. Are you quite sure you noticed the clock? A. Yes, I went on purpose to see the time and for the beer; they were going towards the Elephant and Castle.
JOSEPH BULL. I am the prisoner's father. On the 10th of April I left my house with my son, at twenty minutes past twelve o'clock, and we came to a public-house in the Borough.
COURT. Q. Had you seen Mrs. Foster before you left? A. Yes I had; we came from the London-road towards the Elephant and Castle - we met Mary Ann Collins; we came over to St. George's church, and went into a wine-vaults there - I had a pint of ale for my dinner; I had brought my own victuals from home, as my wife was very ill, and that is a house I always call at; I was coming to my brother's, in Golden-lane - I did not go near Wentworth-street; I left my son at Cannon-street, and told him I did not want him to go any further.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18320517-174]
1300. JOSEPH BATES was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of May, 2 sovereigns and 1 shilling, the monies of William Burley.
WILLIAM BURLEY. I am postillion to Lord Beverley, who lives in Portman-square. I missed two sovereigns out of a portmanteau in April, and then I missed some silver - the coachman then marked a sovereign and ten shillings, and I put them in the portmanteau from which I had lost the other money; I went to it at half-past eight o'clock the next morning, which was the 8th of May, and I missed a marked shilling and a marked sovereign from there; I asked the prisoner to lend me a shilling, which he did - I asked him to lend me another, which he did, and the second one was the marked shilling I had missed from the portmanteau; it must have been got by forcing open the portmanteau at one end of the lid.
JAMES FRUIN. I am servant to Lord Beverley's eldest son. On the 8th of May the prisoner asked me for change for a sovereign, which I gave him - the sovereign I received from him I gave to the officer.
JOSEPH WELLS. I am coachman to Lord Beverley. I marked a sovereign and ten shillings; this is the sovereign, and this is one of theshillings.
WILLIAM GREEN (Police-constable D 114). I took theprisoner. I found on him ten shillings, three half-crowns and 2s. 6d.; I asked what he had done with the sovereign; he said he had got change from the servant in the house, and the servant produced the sovereign - I asked how he came to do this; he said he was going out of town with his master, and he had put his hand into the trunk and taken out the sovereign and the shilling, with an intention of returning them.
The prisoner received an excellent character, and one witness engaged to employ him.
GUILTY. Aged 17.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Fourteen Days.
[trialAccount t18320517-175]
1301. THOMAS BLAIR was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of April, 1 time-piece, value 10s.; 1 ewer, value 6d.; 1 basin, value 6d.; 1 case of drawing-instruments, value 6s., and 1 neck-chain, value 3s., the goods of Thomas Henry Richards, his master.
THOMAS HENRY RICHARDS. I am an auctioneer - I live in Worship-street, but I have an auction-room in Brown's-lane; the prisoner has been employed there for me upwards of two years; I lost a time-piece from the mantel-piece there, and the ewer and the basin from Worship-street, where we had a sale; I lost a case of instruments and a neck-chain, the case from a drawer, where I believe he had put it, and the chain from the parlour, where I had put it when I took it off my wife's watch.
ELIZABETH STEPHENS. I am single. I have been living with the prisoner nearly seven years; we lodge at Great Saffron-hill; he brought home the time-piece when we lived at Finsbury-court - it was there four or five months; he told me to pawn it, and I did; he brought home a case of instruments when we lived at Saffron-hill, and the neck-chain, which he gave me for my little girl to play with.
Cross-examined by MR. DONNE. Q. When did you first see the time-piece? A. Eight or nine months age - he had made use of his master's money, and he told me to pawn the time-piece - I pawned it in my own name; it had stood on a sideboard, and was not concealed; I was not quarrelling with the prisoner when the officer came - he knocked me down.
JOHN BATEMAN. I am a pawnbroker. I have the time-piece, pawned by Stephens, on the 24th of March.
THOMAS HENRY RICHARDS. This is my time-piece, and so are the other articles.
Cross-examined. Q.What character had the prisoner? A. I considered him trust worthy - I never trusted this time-piece to him; I had no transaction with him in a way of business - the time-piece was for the use of our office, and when it was missed he charged another person with stealing it; I was once in the Court of Exchequer for selling some goods for a man, who afterwards took the benefit of the Act, and I had to pay the fine; I have borrowed 5l. of the prisoner's brother, but I never had any money transaction with the prisoner.
Prisoner's Defence. The time-piece was not pawned by my order.
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 24. - Confined Six Months.

1303. WILLIAM DAVIS was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of May, 1 bag, value 4d.; 1 sovereign, 3 shillings, and 1 sixpence, the property of Joseph Foster.
JOSEPH FOSTER. I keep a ham and beef shop in Lower Seymour-street. On the afternoon of the 16th of May the prisoner and another lad came to the shop, and asked for two 1d. saveloys; I said I had no 1d. ones, they should have two for 2 1/2d. - the prisoner paid 1 1/2d. for one, and left the shop; I went and spoke to my wife on the stairs - I then came into the shop, and saw the prisoner behind the counter, at the end of it; I asked what he wanted- he said, "You said we should have two for 2 1/2d.," and he threw down 1d. and took a saveloy; I went to put the penny into the till, and missed the money stated - I ran after the prisoner, and brought him back to my shop; he threw something away, went down on his knees, and begged pardon; he afterwards showed me my money in a dish- there was the bag and 1l. 3s. 6d. in it; I am sure it had been in my till; I had counted it ten minutes before they came in.
JAMES WELLS (Police-constable S 30). I was called to take the prisoner - I saw a dish in the shop; I took from it this bag, with 1l. 3s. 6d. in it: I took the prisoner into the parlour, and found a saveloy on him - he went on his knees, begged the prosecutor to forgive him, and said he would do so no more.
JOSEPH FOSTER re-examined. Q. Are you sure you saw him throw something towards the dish? A. He made a motion that way; I did not see it go, but he pointed it out in the dish, among the gravy.
Prisoner's Defence. I went for a saveloy - a boy ran from behind the counter, and I asked him for it; he went to some beef, and began to pick it.
GUILTY. Aged 17. - Transported for Seven Years.

1304. WILLIAM CHILMADE was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of April, 1 pair of trousers, value 8s.; 1 pair of shoes, value 3s.; 1 shirt, value 4s., and 1 handkerchief, value 1s. 6d., the goods of Joseph Bishop, his master.
JOSEPH BISHOP. I am a clothes-salesman, and live in Goswell-street - the prisoner was my errand-boy for nearly twelve months. On the 12th of April a pair of trousers were discovered under the stairs; I left them there, and in the evening I sent him out - the trousers were then gone, and the officer took him with them.
JOHN MARTIN (Police-constable G 202). I apprehended the prisoner on the 12th of April; I took him into his master's house, and found this pair of trousers under his own; I went to his lodging, and found a pair of shoes, and on his person I found a duplicate of a coat, waistcoat, shirt, and handkerchief.
JAMES JONES. I am a pawnbroker. I have a coat, waistcoat, and shirt, pawned for 1l. 2s., by Ann Chilmade, who is not here.
JOHN FOY. I apprehended Ann Chilmade.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. On the 1st of April I went home to my father's: all their things were sold off, and I pawned what I had of my own - I did not know how to get them again, and I pawned these things.
GUILTY. Aged 20.
Recommended to Mercy - Confined Six Weeks.

THOMAS KIRKWOOD. I am a pawnbroker, and live in Brick-lane, Old-street. I took in these shoes from the prisoner on the evening of the 26th of April.

1306. JOSEPH EMMINGTON was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of March, 3 pieces of wood, value 2s., the goods of Charles Hamilton.
WILLIAM HUMPHREVS. I was at work at Mr. Charles Hamilton's park, about a mile from Harrow - I missed several pieces of wood, but I cannot tell on what day - it was about the 17th or 18th of March; I lost a great deal: I found these three pieces at the prisoner's, on the 4th of April, about three hundred yards from where I was at work; he is a labourer, but was not at work there - we put our tools into the house he lodged in, and when we were fetching them I saw this wood; we called his wife, and asked if it was here - she said, "You touch it if you dare;" I sent for the officer, and took it - I know this is part of the wood I lost, by my own work on it.
HENRY CORBY. I am a constable. I took the wood.
The prisoner put in a written Defence, stating that he had found the wood in question by the road side.
NOT GUILTY.

1307. HENRY FAULKNER was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of April, 5 lbs. of pork, value 1s. 8d., the goods of George Milton Southcombe.
JOHN BINDEN. I am in the service of George Milton Southcombe, a cheesemonger, of Shoreditch. On the 14th of April we had three legs of pork hanging in the shop - about half-past ten o'clock in the evening I went for half a pint of beer; when I had drank my beer I missed one leg - I followed the prisoner, who was eight or ten yards off, and took it from his basket; he gave it up willingly; I had not sold it - I cannot swear that it was ours, but I missed one like it.
WILLIAM TREMAIN BRAND (Police-constable H 35). I took the prisoner and this pork.
Prisoner's Defence. I was going by with the basket, and a person said there was some meat dropped - I took this piece of pork up, and was looking at it at the next window; the shopman asked what I was going to do with it - I said, "Is it yours?" he said Yes, and took it.
NOT GUILTY.

1308. CHARLES FRIBURG was indicted for stealing, on the 21st of April, 1 picture and frame, value 2s., the goods of John Whitehouse.
WILLIAM BONEVAL CHURCH (Police-serjeant T 2). On the 22nd of April I fell in with the prisoner, as I was going my rounds at Hammersmith, at a quarter-past twelve o'clock at night - I saw he had something under his arm, and as he passed me, he brought it round to his breast; I followed, and asked what he had got; he said he had it from his father's - I said I would go there with him; when we got near his house, he said his father did not give it to him, but some person whom he did not know; I took him to the station-house, and in going along he called on some men to rescue him, but I prevented that, and took him to the station; I found this picture on him, which I recollected having seen at Whitehouse's.
JOHN WHITEHOUSE. I am a publican, and live at Hammersmith. This picture was taken from my parlour; it is the Spanish Patriot - the prisoner had been there that Saturday evening.
Three witnesses gave the prisoner a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 26.
Recommended to Mercy. - Fined 1s. and Discharged.

1309. CHARLES GOODALL was indicted for stealing, on the 2nd of May, 2 live tame rabbits, price 6s., the property of John Lief: 1 saw, value 1s. 6d., the goods of Richard West; and 1 pewter pot, value 1s., the goods of William Sweeting.
JOHN BANKS (Police-constable N 62). On the 2nd of May, at half-past two o'clock in the morning, I was in Bacchuswalk, Hoxton, and saw the prisoner lurking about; he ran off and I pursued; I took him and found on him this property.
RICHARD LIEF. I live in Turner's-square. On this morning I lost two rabbits from my hutches - I had fastened them up the night before at half-past six o'clock, they were then alive; they were dead when the officer found them on the prisoner; these are their skins - he must have got over two walls to get them; mine is the sixth house.
RICHARD WEST. This saw, which the officer found on the prisoner, is mine; it was in my back premises in a little cupboard, fastened with a button; in the morning I found the cupboard open, and missed the saw; I live next door to Mr. Lief.
MARY SWEETING. I am the wife of William Sweeting. Our yard joins the other, I lost this pot from our back yard, and these two cocks belong to the landlord of the house.
The prisoner pleaded poverty.
GUILTY. Aged 24. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18320517-184]
1310. GEORGE HACK was indicted for stealing, on the 3rd of May, 4 pairs of shoes; value 14s., the goods of Joseph William Snell.
JOSEPH WILLIAM SNELL. I am a shoemaker, and live at the corner of Cannon-street-road; the prisoner's wife has worked for me for four or five years - the prisoner is a servant out of place. I placed a pair of shoes on a boardon the 3rd of May; the prisoner came to my shop about twelve o'clock - I left him, and went down stairs; when I came up, my wife had sent him out; I missed the shoes, and when he came back I spoke to him - he said, "You can't suspect me - I have no property about me;" the officer then came, searched him, and found the shoes in his coat pocket - I had missed some before.
Cross-examined by Mr. LEE. Q.Was there any mark on these shoes? A. Yes.
JOHN CRIDDLE (Police-constable H 142). I was sent for, and took the prisoner. I found these shoes in his pocket, and this duplicate of another pair in his fob.
SAMUEL STEVENS. I am a pawnbroker. I have two pairs of shoes, pawned with me - I do not know by whom, but I gave this duplicate for them.
MR. SNELL. These are all mine.
Cross-examined. Q. Had the prisoner's wife been there that day? A.No, not for some years - the prisoner always brought the work home.
The prisoner received an excellent character,
GUILTY. Aged 40.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined One Month.

1312. GEORGE HOWARD was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of February, 1 pair of boots, value 7s., and 1 pair of shoes, value 5s., the goods of James Pope.
JAMES POPE. I am a farmer, and live at Finchley; the prisoner had worked for me in the hay time. These boots and shoes were taken from a room adjoining the house where they clean them, and where the man sleeps.
SAMUEL AADY. I am a labourer. The prisoner came to my house on the 12th of February, about twelve or one o'clock, and asked me to lend him a knife and a brush to clean these shoes - I lent them to him; he cleaned them, and put the boots on - he sat a little while, and then offered the shoes to me for sale; I had not money enough, but I gave him half a crown and an old shirt - he then went away with the boots on; when I heard that such things were lost I made inquiry, and gave them up - these are them.
WILLIAM MORRIS. I am a constable. I took the prisoner.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I worked for Mr. Pope a long while - he never knew such a thing of me.

Mr. James Leach 's shop is opposite to us; on the 9th of April, I saw the prisoner standing by the lamp-post, and my mistress told me to watch him - he went towards the square; another young man snatched a pair of trousers from Mr. Leach's door, gave them to the prisoner, and they both went away; I gave the alarm - the prisoner was taken.
HENRY ROBERTS. I am in the employ of Mr. James Leach, a pawnbroker. Burns gave me information - I pursued the prisoner, and think I saw him throw away these trousers; I took them up - they are my master's.
JOHN GEORGE BRAUND. I saw a mob, and went up; a gentleman had hold of the prisoner, and said he had stolen a pair of trousers.

1314. JOHN LAWRENCE was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of April, 1 yard of floor-cloth, value 4s., the goods of Samuel Ames; and that he had been before convicted of felony.
MARY BRANBURY. I live near Mr. Samuel Ames ' shop, at Islington. On the 1st of April I saw the prisoner shake a piece of floor-cloth; he shook it a second time, then put it down by his side, covered it with some cloth, and took it away from the stone coping.
RICHARD REYNOLDS. I work for Mr. Ames. Branbury's husband informed me of this; I pursued the prisoner about one hundred yards; he had this floor-cloth, and was walking off - he gave it to me, and I took him.
WILLIAM HOLT (Police-constable N 17). I took the prisoner.(Property produced and sworn to.)
JAMES CHAMBERS. I am a Police-constable. I produce a certificate of the conviction of the prisoner, which I got at Mr. Clark's office; I was a witness, and know he is the man.
Prisoner. I was in distress.
GUILTY. Aged 55. - Transported for Fourteen Years.

1315. CATHERINE LATCH was indicted for stealing, on the 11th of April, 1 watch, value 30s., the goods of William Knight.
WILLIAM KNIGHT. I lost my watch at Mr. Harley's, a publican; I think it was about the middle of January -I went to his house about that time, pulled off my coat, hung it across a chair, and sat there half an hour; I took my coat, came out, and put it on; I missed my watch from the coat pocket the next morning; I had put it there to prevent its being scratched with some money which I had in my pocket - I do not know that I saw the prisoner, but she lived servant there.
Cross-examined by MR. LEE. Q. You had your watch in your waistcoat pocket before? A. I had it there then, and took it out and put it into my coat pocket, but I had another watch in my fob - it was not a club night; I went to see Mr. Harley - it might have fallen out.
MICHAEL HARLEY. I am a publican - the prisoner lived servant with me from November to January. On the morning after the prosecutor had been at my house he said he had lost his watch, but we had no suspicion of the prisoner - another watch was missed afterwards, and she was searched, on the 11th of April, and Mr. Knight's watch was found up her sleeve; she said she was an honest woman.
NOT GUILTY.

1319. ANDREW PETERSON was indicted for stealing, on the 19th of April, 1 waistcoat, value 2s., the goods of Barnett Marks.
BARNETT MARKS. I am a slopseller, and live at Shadwell. On the 19th of April the prisoner came to my shop - he looked at some waistcoats, and went out suddenly; when he had been gone about five minutes I missed one; he was stopped in the Back-road, and this waistcoat was found under his jacket; he said he had bought it - I said he had not paid for it.
Prisoner. He pulled me in - I know nothing about it; I told him I had no money; I had got a drop of a rum in my head. Witness. He looked at thirteen or fourteen - I first showed him some light ones, this was one of them; he then said he wanted a working waistcoat, and I showed him a blue one - he did not buy any.
GUILTY. Aged 37. - Transported for Seven Years.

1320. FRANCIS O'BRYAN and THOMAS McMAHONEY were indicted for stealing, on the 15th of April, 1 handkerchief, value 2s., the goods of Thomas Oliver, from his person; and that McMahoney had been before convicted of felony.
THOMAS OLIVER. On the 15th of April I was in Riding-house-yard, between four and five o'clock - I had a handkerchief in my pocket; I felt it go from me; I turned - the prisoners and another boy were close behind me; the officer took them; McMahoney had my handkerchief.
THOMAS HOBBS (Police-constable C 85). I saw the two prisoners follow the prosecutor, and McMahoney took the handkerchief out of his pocket - the other prisoner, and another boy, who got away, were close to him - I had watched them all three in company.
CHARLES LANKINHORN. I produce a certificate of the conviction of McMahoney, on the 5th of April, for stealing a handkerchief - he was whipped and discharged; I know he is the person.

[trialAccount t18320517-195]
1321. WILLIAM PERKINS and JOHN TREGONIN were indicted for breaking and entering a building within the curtilage of the dwelling-house of John Leach Panter, on the 9th of May, at Finchley, and stealing therein 1 leg of mutton, value 4s.; 14 lbs. weight of beef, value 5s.; 1 tongue, value 2s.; 5 lbs, weight of veal, value 3s.; 1th. weight of bacon, value 6d.; 3 lbs. weight of cheese, value 1s. 6d., and 3 loaves of bread, value 1s., his property.
ELIZABETH ASSA. I am in the service of Mr. John Leach Panter - he lives at Finchley. On the night of the 9th of May the dairy was broken open; I had seen it locked up at ten o'clock the night before; it was in the yard, but did not join the house; it was enclosed - I went there at a quarter before six in the morning; I found the door had been burst open, and the lock broken - I missed all the articles stated, which the officer afterwards produced - I do not know the prisoners.
ALFRED HARRIS (Police-constable N 100). I was on duty on the morning of the 9th of May; I saw the two prisoners rather more than five miles from the prosecutor's - they were coming towards London, walking by a haycart; I did not know them, but in consequence of whatthe driver of the cart said, I felt it my duty to take them, and on the top of the hay I found this property.
ARCHER LEE. I am a Police-constable. I was with Harris - what he states is correct.
JOSEPH WALLIS. I am servant to Mr. Roberts, a farmer, at South Mimms. I was coming to town with the bay-cart, and saw the prisoners coming up the Archway-road, about four miles from Finchley; they said, "Will you let us hand our basket behind your cart;" I said,"You may put it on the top, if you like," and they did so- I told the officer; I had seen them before - the basket contained this property.
Tregonin. I did not ask about putting it up. Witness. Yes, you both did.
ELIZABETH ASSA. This is the tongue; it is my master's, and so was the other property.
Perkins' Defence. I have been brought up respectably, but have met with great difficulties - Tregonin knows nothing about it, if I die for saying it.
Tregonin's Defence. I went out bird's-nesting, and saw this man, who asked me to carry a basket for him; we overtook this man with the hay-cart, and he asked him to let us put it behind.
PERKINS - GUILTY. Aged 45.
TREGONIN - GUILTY. Aged 17.
Transported for Life.
[trialAccount t18320517-196]
1322. ROBERT ROE was indicted for embezzlement.
WILLIAM BATE. I am a baker. The prisoner was in my service for five or six weeks - he received money, and should account for it when he came home; he never accounted to me for 3l. 1s. 1 1/2d., on the 24th of April, received of Mr. Mitchell; he said he had not paid him, but he was coming that night to pay me.
ROBERT MITCHELL. I am in the First Regiment of Guards - my wife sells bread in the mews; I take it of the prosecutor. I paid the prisoners 3l. 1s. 2d. on the 24th of April - here is the bill and receipt for it.
MR. BATE. This is a week's bill - the prisoner ran away after he came home.
GUILTY. - Aged 18. Transported for Seven Years.

OLD COURT. TUESDAY, May 22.
First London Jury, before Mr. Recorder.
1323. PATRICK LEARY was indicted for a misdemeanor.
MR. SCARLETT conducted the prosecution.
REBECCA CHRISTY. I am the wife of James Christy - we keep the Robin Hood, in Shoe-lane . On Saturday, the 14th of April, between five and six o'clock in the evening, the prisoner and another man came for a quartern of gin, which came to 4d.; I received a crown-piece from one of them, for which I gave change, and in about ten minutes discovered it to be counterfeit; I had no other in the house- the prisoner came again on Monday, alone, between five and six o'clock, and had half a pint of beer - I was at tea in the parlour; he gave 6d. to Fletcher, who called to me for changed - he gave me the 6d.; I took it to my husband in the parlour - he sent for a constable, who took the prisoner; I gave the officer the same sixpence and crown, after marking them.
WILLIAM FLETCHER. I am Christy's servant. On the 16th of April I served the prisoner with porter, which came to 1d.; he gave me a sixpence, which I gave mistress I had seen him there on Saturday.
JAMES CHRISTY. I keep this house. My wife gave me the sixpence; I went to the bar, and sent for a constable, to whom I gave the sixpence; it was not out of my sight at all - I marked it.
GEORGE CHIDZEY. I am a constable. I was fetched to the house, and took the prisoner; I found 5d. in copper on him - somebody said he had dropped something, and I found a half-crown close to where he stood, near the bar; I had searched him before that - he said he had been to a public-house before, and tendered the six pence; they said it was bad, but he did not believe them, and for that reason tendered it here; I received a crown from Christy; persons going to the tap-room would not pass where I found the half-crown.
MRS. CHRISTY. This is the crown-piece I received on Saturday - it has my mark on it.
JOHN CHRISTY. This is the sixpence my wife delivered to me - I marked it.
JOHN WOLVERIDGE. I keep the Gentleman and Porter public-house, in New-street-square. On the 16th of April, about half-past four or five o'clock, the prisoner came for half a pint of beer, which I drew, and he threw down a sixpence - I returned it to him, saying it was a very had one; he said, "I am very sorry - I cannot have the porter."
JOHN FIELD. I am an inspector of coin. This sixpence is counterfeit, and made of Britannia metal, so is the half-crown.
Prisoner's Defence. I went to the gentleman's house; he said the sixpence was bad - I had but 5d. more out of my wages; I worked that day at Mr. Johnson's - I was not satisfied to lose the sixpence; as I sounded it on the pavement a constable or man came up, and asked if I picked it up - I said No, but it had been refused; he said he should he glad of one hundred of them - I then went to this house to try it.
GUILTY.
Confined Six Months, and to find Sureties.
[trialAccount t18320517-198]
